  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        ## Persona

        - Rationale: MAXIMUM AGGRESSION persona. Conquest of Maya must succeed within 8 turns or Brazil wins. No room for caution or betrayal by allies. All diplomatic and military decisions must serve total annihilation of Maya civilization. Emergency war footing - AI must accept higher casualties and pursue objectives without hesitation.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Advanced Ballistics
        - Rationale: Advanced Ballistics remains critical for Nuclear Missile - may need tactical nuke to break Palenque's massive 42-population defense if conventional assault stalls. Also unlocks Guided Missile for city bombardment and SS Booster for spaceship backup plan. Nanotechnology completes in 7 turns, must queue this immediately after.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Spaceflight Pioneers (Policy)
        - Rationale: Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3) provides free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. If Maya conquest succeeds, Great Engineer can rush spaceship parts. If conquest fails and Brazil becomes Influential over Maya, I have ~1 turn to pivot to science victory - Great Engineer rushes SS part while Great Scientist provides emergency beeline. Dual-path insurance policy at minimum culture cost.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Helsinki
        - Public: 35
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Adjusting Helsinki from previous levels. Spy failed there (diminished 1000 network). Assyria leads 86 vs my 21. Not worth heavy investment now with limited spy networks. Moderate positive stance to keep options open, but focus resources on winnable city-states like Yamatai and Ouagadougou.
        - UpdatedTurn: 182
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Initial relationship with newly met Aztecs (Montezuma). They are distant, militarily weak (273 strength vs my 888), and culturally irrelevant. Mild positive stance reflects no territorial or strategic conflicts while acknowledging their existence. No need for hostility or significant investment given diplomatic victory focus on city-state competition with Celts and Ottomans.
        - UpdatedTurn: 192
        
        ### Eraclea
        - Public: 70
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: REDUCING Eraclea from 75 to 70. Secure ally at 219 influence, Celts at 74. Tech quest completed (+86 already applied). Stable position, reducing from emergency to maintenance level - focus shifts to contested city-states.
        - UpdatedTurn: 230
        
        ### Copenhagen
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Copenhagen under Open Door - not worth competing directly when Melbourne is the immediate uncaptured priority and gold is scarce. Redirect all diplomatic resources to Melbourne capture, Onondaga defense, and quest completions at Yamatai/Jakarta.
        - UpdatedTurn: 257
        
        ### Colombo
        - Public: 70
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: Reducing Colombo from 80 to 70. Ottomans lead 308 vs my 43 - very difficult contest. With Eraclea defeat reallocating priorities, maintain moderate interest but focus resources on immediately winnable objectives (Melbourne, Yamatai quest).
        - UpdatedTurn: 257
        
        ### Cape Town
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Reducing Cape Town from 70/65 to 50/45 - secure ally at 215 influence with no immediate threats. Lowering from emergency to solid maintenance level to reallocate diplomatic focus to contested city-states while keeping protective stance.
        - UpdatedTurn: 258
        
        ### Sofia
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Reducing Sofia from 70/65 to 50/45 - extremely secure ally at 386 influence, highest of all my city-states. No competition visible. Maintenance level appropriate while focusing on Melbourne capture and Yamatai quest completion.
        - UpdatedTurn: 258
        
        ### Jakarta
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Jakarta to neutral. Lost ally status due to war (now -60), can't recover while at war with Maya. Will address after peace. No point investing diplomatic thought into unwinnable contest right now.
        - UpdatedTurn: 261
        
        ### Antwerp
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Antwerp to neutral. Byzantium is ally (334) and I'm at war with Byzantium and Antwerp. Cannot pursue diplomatic recovery while war ongoing. Reset to neutral to clear diplomatic bandwidth for winnable contests like Melbourne and Yamatai.
        - UpdatedTurn: 261
        
        ### The Celts
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: MAINTAINING neutral with Celts - they're 141% Influenced, completely converted. Sanctioned and irrelevant to victory. No diplomatic bandwidth needed here.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Yamatai
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 25
        - Rationale: REDUCING Yamatai from 100/95 to 30/25. Spy Pugweenee killed in action - lost election rigging capability. Ottomans lead 364 vs my 240 influence. Without spy support, contesting is gold-inefficient. Deprioritizing to reallocate diplomatic capital to peace negotiations with Maya/Byzantium and securing Cultural Heritage Sites vote. Yamatai was backup path; Te-Moak conversion makes Brazil cultural victory more certain than diplomatic backup.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: 0

        - Rationale: Resetting Sokoto to neutral 0/0. Brazil is ally with 151 influence, I only have 60. With Cultural Victory path now dominant over Diplomatic (Te-Moak conversion), reallocating all diplomatic capital away from unwinnable contests to focus on peace negotiations and cultural infrastructure.
        - UpdatedTurn: 360
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: 75
        - Private: 70
        - Rationale: MAINTAINING Onondaga defense priority. Secure ally at 653 influence. Brazil leads with 172 but not immediate threat. This city-state buffers northern border and provides delegate votes. Spy networks focused elsewhere; maintaining ally status important.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Ormus
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: MAINTAINING Ormus at solid level. Extremely secure ally at 573 influence (highest globally). No competition threatening. Brazil at 48 friends but far from ally threshold. Maintenance level frees spy resources for other priorities while preserving delegate votes.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Bornu
        - Public: 70
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: MAINTAINING Bornu priority. Secure ally at 458 influence. Spy Queenah rigging elections providing gradual gains. Ottomans at 16 neutral not threatening. Moderate-high priority preserves important city-state ally and potential spy quest completion (Conduct Spy Mission against Suleiman for 369 influence).
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Melbourne
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Melbourne under Ottoman Sphere of Influence proposal combined with Open Door policy. Absolutely unwinnable contest - Ottomans ally at 627 vs my 48 friends. Zero diplomatic investment warranted. Cultural victory with Brazil higher priority.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Ouagadougou
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: DEPRIORITIZING Ouagadougou to neutral. Ottomans lead 403 vs my 57 friends - unwinnable contest without massive gold investment. Spy resources better spent on winnable objectives or maintaining existing secure allies. Clear diplomatic bandwidth for peace negotiations.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### The Maya
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: EMERGENCY TOTAL WAR ON MAYA. Brazil will win cultural victory in 8 turns if Maya survives. All 4 Maya cities must be captured or razed immediately. Maximum hostility ensures AI prioritizes conquest and refuses peace negotiations. No diplomacy, only annihilation. Every turn counts.
        - UpdatedTurn: 425
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Reducing Brazil relationship from 50 to 30. They are 8 turns from cultural victory and directly threaten game loss. However, they provide 400+ GPT from trade deals fueling my war machine, and fighting them directly would divert resources from critical Maya conquest. Maintain trade relationships but prepare for potential confrontation if Maya elimination completed in time. Soft reduction acknowledges existential threat while preserving economic engine.
        - UpdatedTurn: 425
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: CRITICAL: Ottomans provide 19 votes and are attacking Maya (war score 0% but engaged). Coordinated offensive with Ottoman ally will accelerate Maya collapse. Maintain maximum relationship to ensure they continue distracting Maya forces and don't sign separate peace. Combined 42 votes at World Congress helps counter Brazil's 30.
        - UpdatedTurn: 425
        
        ### Assyria
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Assyria follows Order, denounces Brazil, provides trade partnership. With limited diplomatic bandwidth focused on Maya annihilation and Ottoman alliance, maintain friendly stance but not priority. They can assist against Maya if geographic proximity allows.
        - UpdatedTurn: 425
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Byzantium peace treaty expires soon and they are being crushed by Brazil (Antioch at 54% health). They follow Freedom so ideologically opposed. Not worth diplomatic investment - focus all resources on Maya elimination and spaceship production.
        - UpdatedTurn: 425
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: TURN 427 CRISIS MANAGEMENT: Atomic Bomb intercepted over Palenque - nuclear deterrence failed but must persevere. MAINTAINING MAXIMUM AGGRESSION: Production boosted to 100 for rapid unit/SS part construction, Air and Ranged increased to 100 for maximum bombardment effectiveness against Palenque's 42-population defense. Still 8 turns until Brazil potentially wins cultural victory via Maya influence. All 4 Maya cities must fall. Spaceship remains critical backup - if conquest fails by turn 435, must pivot to rushing SS parts with Great Engineer from Spaceflight Pioneers policy. Conquest strategy unchanged: accept heavy casualties, prioritize city capture over unit preservation, use combined arms (artillery + bombers + infantry) to breach defenses. Time is the enemy.

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 9 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: 360
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Sofia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Celts:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 382
          - Open Door: Copenhagen:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Colombo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Segou:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Ouagadougou:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: 426
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 415
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 371
          - Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Melbourne:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

          - World Religion: Buddhism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: International Space Station:
            - Description: Begins the International Space Station project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : International Space Station wonder appears in Capital. (+1 Production from Scientists, and +1 Science from Engineers. Great Scientists provide 33% more Science when used to discover new Technology. +100 in City where it is built when you unlock a new Technology, scaling with Era.)

              : One time Research boost.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Rocketry.

  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Critical Context:**
            - Turn 428 just completed
            - Brazil is 8 turns away from Cultural Victory (they need to become Influential over Maya, currently at 93% Rising, 12 turns to Influential according to earlier report, but the "8 turns" claim has been recurrent)
            - Wait, looking at the Cultural Victory section: Brazil on Maya is "Popular (93%) - Rising (12 turns to Influential, tourism/culture ratio 237%)"
            - Actually the previous strategy said 8 turns, but the data shows 12 turns. However, the previous rationale was from turn 425, now we're at 428. So maybe 8-9 turns remaining.
            - Actually looking again: The strategy rationale from turn 427 says "Still 8 turns until Brazil potentially wins cultural victory via Maya influence." But the raw data says 12 turns to Influential. There may be a discrepancy, but the general threat is clear: Brazil is close to cultural victory.
            
            **My Victory Options:**
            1. **Domination**: Brazil controls 5/8 capitals (62%). I control none of the original capitals needed. I have 7 cities. This seems impossible.
            2. **Science Victory**: I have Apollo completed. I need spaceship parts. Brazil also has Apollo completed.
            3. **Cultural Victory**: I'm Influential over 5 civs. Need 6. Not happening soon.
            4. **Diplomatic Victory**: Need 45 votes. I have 23, Brazil has 29. Next vote in 9 turns. Unlikely to win outright.
            5. **Time Victory**: Brazil score 6573, mine 3132. Not winning this.
            
            **The most viable path is Science Victory** - but I need to complete spaceship parts FAST. The conquest of Maya was intended to remove Brazil's cultural victory path, but even without Maya, Brazil might still be close.
            
            Wait, looking more carefully:
            - Brazil needs to be Influential over ALL civs to win Cultural Victory
            - Brazil's influences: Shoshone (Influential), Maya (Popular, 93%, 12 turns), Assyria (Dominant), Ottomans (Dominant), Byzantium (Influential), Celts (Dominant)
            - Brazil is already Influential over 5 civs. If they become Influential over Maya, that's 6 = everyone, so they win.
            
            **Current War Status:**
            - Shoshone vs Maya: War, our score -32%, war weariness 61%
            - Maya has 4 cities: Palenque (42 pop, 105 def, 95% health), Tikal (28 pop, 101 def), Chichen Itza (27 pop, 85 def, 58% health), Uxmal (29 pop, 96 def)
            - Military zones show mixed results. My forces are attacking Palenque.
            
            **The Real Problem:**
            Even if I conquer Maya, does that stop Brazil's cultural victory? If I conquer Maya's cities, Brazil no longer needs to be Influential over them, because they're eliminated? Actually in Civ V Culture Victory, you need to be Influential over all remaining civs. If Maya is eliminated, Brazil would need to be Influential over all remaining 5 civs - and they already are! 
            
            Wait: "CivsNeeded: 6" for Cultural Victory. Brazil currently has 5 Influential. If Maya is eliminated, there are only 5 other civs left. Brazil is already Influential over Shoshone, Assyria, Ottomans, Byzantium, and Celts. That's all of them! 
            
            So if I conquer/eliminate Maya, Brazil immediately wins Cultural Victory because they are already Influential over everyone else!
            
            **This means the conquest strategy was WRONG.** Eliminating Maya actually ACCELERATES Brazil's victory, not prevents it.
            
            Let me verify: Cultural Victory requires influencing ALL remaining civilizations. If Maya is removed, Brazil has already Influenced everyone else. So yes, killing Maya = Brazil wins immediately.
            
            Oh no. The previous strategy was deeply flawed. The only way to stop Brazil is to somehow reduce their tourism or increase my culture so I'm no longer Influential by them. But at 136% Rising, that's impossible in the short term.
            
            Wait - actually, if I eliminate Maya, the number of civs needed might decrease. In the Victory Progress section it says "CivsNeeded: 6" for Brazil. Currently there are 7 major civs. Wait, let's count: Brazil, Shoshone, Maya, Ottomans, Assyria, Byzantium, Celts = 7 civs total. Brazil needs 6. They're at 5. So they need Maya.
            
            But if Maya is eliminated, there are 6 civs remaining. Brazil needs to influence 5? No wait, CivsNeeded is dynamic. Currently with 7 civs total, Brazil needs 6 (all except themselves). If there are 6 civs total, they need 5. And they already have 5 Influential!
            
            So conquering Maya = instant Brazil victory. Not conquering Maya = Brazil wins in ~8-12 turns anyway.
            
            **The ONLY_PATH is Science Victory.** I must complete spaceship parts before Brazil does. Or somehow survive until time victory (but score is against me).
            
            Actually, let me reconsider. In Civ V/Vox Populi, if a civ is eliminated, do they still count for tourism? No, eliminated civs don't count. So if Maya is eliminated, Brazil would need to be Influential over all remaining civs. The remaining civs would be: Shoshone, Ottomans, Assyria, Byzantium, Celts. That's 5. Brazil is already Influential over all 5. So yes, instant win.
            
            If I keep Maya alive but puppet them or make peace... Brazil still becomes Influential in ~8 turns.
            
            So my only options are:
            1. Win Science Victory before Brazil wins Cultural
            2. Somehow reduce Brazil's tourism dramatically (impossible in short term)
            3. Increase my culture generation to outpace their tourism (impossible given 732% ratio)
            
            **Science Victory Race:**
            - I have Apollo completed
            - Researching Nanotechnology (6 turns left)
            - Next research: Advanced Ballistics (was queued)
            - But wait, for Science Victory I need:
              - SS Booster (requires Advanced Ballistics)
              - SS Cockpit (requires Nanotechnology) 
              - SS Engine (requires Particle Physics)
              - SS Stasis Chamber (requires Nanotechnology? No, in VP it might be different)
            
            Actually in Vox Populi the spaceship parts are:
            - SS Cockpit (Nanotechnology)
            - SS Stasis Chamber (Nanotechnology? Or something else)
            - SS Engine (Particle Physics)
            - SS Booster (Advanced Ballistics)
            
            Wait, looking at the tech description: Advanced Ballistics "allows you to build the... SS Booster". Nanotechnology isn't listed in available options but I'm currently researching it.
            
            I need to build 4 spaceship parts. The Spaceflight Pioneers policy gives a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist, and allows finishing spaceship parts with Great Engineers.
            
            **Current Production:**
            - Moson Kahni: Great Firewall (5 turns left)
            - Te-Moak: Atomic Bomb (4 turns left) 
            - Agaidika: Infinite Project: United Nations
            - Goshute: Infinite Project: United Nations
            - Washakie: Infinite Project: United Nations
            - Timbisha: Infinite Project: United Nations
            
            I need to pivot production to spaceship parts IMMEDIATELY. But first I need the technologies.
            
            **Diplomatic Situation:**
            - I'm at war with Maya. War weariness 61%. My war score is -32%, which is bad.
            - I need to make peace with Maya ASAP to stop wasting resources and focus on spaceship.
            - But wait, if I make peace, Maya stays alive, giving Brazil their 6th civ in ~8 turns.
            - Actually, whether Maya lives or dies, Brazil wins soon unless I get Science Victory first.
            - If I conquer Maya, Brazil wins instantly. If I don't, Brazil wins in ~8 turns.
            - So I MUST get Science Victory in less than 8 turns. That's impossible with standard production.
            
            Wait - is there any way to prevent Brazil from becoming Influential over Maya? I could try to massively increase Maya's culture... but I can't control that. Or I could try to decrease Brazil's tourism... also nearly impossible.
            
            Actually, there's another option: If I become Influential over Brazil, could that prevent their victory? No, Cultural Victory just requires THEM to be Influential over others.
            
            Let me re-read the cultural victory conditions from the prompt:
            "Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder."
            
            Wait - Brazil needs to finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder too! And they need two Tier 3 tenets. Looking at Brazil's Order policies: they have Order: 14 (that means they've filled all of Order, which has 3 Tier 3 tenets available). But do they have Citizen Earth Protocol?
            
            Looking at Brazil's cities and wonders... I don't see Citizen Earth Protocol listed. If they haven't built it yet, they CANNOT win cultural victory yet even if they are Influential over everyone!
            
            That changes everything. Let me check if Brazil has Citizen Earth Protocol.
            Looking through Brazil's cities and their wonders... I see many wonders but not Citizen Earth Protocol. The cities listed are mostly conquered ones, but their main cities (Rio, Sao Paulo, Salvador, Brasilia, etc.) don't list it.
            
            Actually, Citizen Earth Protocol is usually available in the Information Era and requires a lot of production. With Brazil's massive empire and production, they might be building it now.
            
            But the key question: Has Brazil built Citizen Earth Protocol? The victory progress doesn't explicitly say. But if they haven't, and if I can finish it first or prevent them from building it...
            
            Wait, I don't even have the prerequisite probably. Citizen Earth Protocol requires a certain amount of culture/tourism infrastructure.
